        int main(void) {

        /* With some toolchains (MSYS2/mingw for example) the compiler
         * provides various builtins which are not really implemented and
         * fall back to the stdlib where they aren't provided and fail at
         * build/link time. In case the user provides a header, including
         * the header didn't lead to the function being defined, and the
         * function we are checking isn't a builtin itself we assume the
         * builtin is not functional and we just error out. */
        #if !1 && !defined(bswap32) && !0
            #error "No definition for __builtin_bswap32 found in the prefix"
        #endif

        #ifdef __has_builtin
            #if !__has_builtin(__builtin_bswap32)
                #error "__builtin_bswap32 not found"
            #endif
        #elif ! defined(bswap32)
            __builtin_bswap32;
        #endif
        return 0;
        }
-----------

        int main(void) {

        /* With some toolchains (MSYS2/mingw for example) the compiler
         * provides various builtins which are not really implemented and
         * fall back to the stdlib where they aren't provided and fail at
         * build/link time. In case the user provides a header, including
         * the header didn't lead to the function being defined, and the
         * function we are checking isn't a builtin itself we assume the
         * builtin is not functional and we just error out. */
        #if !1 && !defined(__builtin_ia32_clflushopt) && !1
            #error "No definition for __builtin_ia32_clflushopt found in the prefix"
        #endif

        #ifdef __has_builtin
            #if !__has_builtin(__builtin_ia32_clflushopt)
                #error "__builtin_ia32_clflushopt not found"
            #endif
        #elif ! defined(__builtin_ia32_clflushopt)
            __builtin_ia32_clflushopt;
        #endif
        return 0;
        }

        int main(void) {

        /* With some toolchains (MSYS2/mingw for example) the compiler
         * provides various builtins which are not really implemented and
         * fall back to the stdlib where they aren't provided and fail at
         * build/link time. In case the user provides a header, including
         * the header didn't lead to the function being defined, and the
         * function we are checking isn't a builtin itself we assume the
         * builtin is not functional and we just error out. */
        #if !1 && !defined(qsort_s) && !0
            #error "No definition for __builtin_qsort_s found in the prefix"
        #endif

        #ifdef __has_builtin
            #if !__has_builtin(__builtin_qsort_s)
                #error "__builtin_qsort_s not found"
            #endif
        #elif ! defined(qsort_s)
            __builtin_qsort_s;
        #endif
        return 0;
        }

        int main(void) {

        /* With some toolchains (MSYS2/mingw for example) the compiler
         * provides various builtins which are not really implemented and
         * fall back to the stdlib where they aren't provided and fail at
         * build/link time. In case the user provides a header, including
         * the header didn't lead to the function being defined, and the
         * function we are checking isn't a builtin itself we assume the
         * builtin is not functional and we just error out. */
        #if !1 && !defined(chflags) && !0
            #error "No definition for __builtin_chflags found in the prefix"
        #endif

        #ifdef __has_builtin
            #if !__has_builtin(__builtin_chflags)
                #error "__builtin_chflags not found"
            #endif
        #elif ! defined(chflags)
            __builtin_chflags;
        #endif
        return 0;
        }

Code:

        int main(void) {

        /* With some toolchains (MSYS2/mingw for example) the compiler
         * provides various builtins which are not really implemented and
         * fall back to the stdlib where they aren't provided and fail at
         * build/link time. In case the user provides a header, including
         * the header didn't lead to the function being defined, and the
         * function we are checking isn't a builtin itself we assume the
         * builtin is not functional and we just error out. */
        #if !1 && !defined(fchflags) && !0
            #error "No definition for __builtin_fchflags found in the prefix"
        #endif

        #ifdef __has_builtin
            #if !__has_builtin(__builtin_fchflags)
                #error "__builtin_fchflags not found"
            #endif
        #elif ! defined(fchflags)
            __builtin_fchflags;
        #endif
        return 0;
        }
-----------

        int main(void) {

        /* With some toolchains (MSYS2/mingw for example) the compiler
         * provides various builtins which are not really implemented and
         * fall back to the stdlib where they aren't provided and fail at
         * build/link time. In case the user provides a header, including
         * the header didn't lead to the function being defined, and the
         * function we are checking isn't a builtin itself we assume the
         * builtin is not functional and we just error out. */
        #if !1 && !defined(getvfsbyname) && !0
            #error "No definition for __builtin_getvfsbyname found in the prefix"
        #endif

        #ifdef __has_builtin
            #if !__has_builtin(__builtin_getvfsbyname)
                #error "__builtin_getvfsbyname not found"
            #endif
        #elif ! defined(getvfsbyname)
            __builtin_getvfsbyname;
        #endif
        return 0;
        }

        int main(void) {

        /* With some toolchains (MSYS2/mingw for example) the compiler
         * provides various builtins which are not really implemented and
         * fall back to the stdlib where they aren't provided and fail at
         * build/link time. In case the user provides a header, including
         * the header didn't lead to the function being defined, and the
         * function we are checking isn't a builtin itself we assume the
         * builtin is not functional and we just error out. */
        #if !1 && !defined(readpassphrase) && !0
            #error "No definition for __builtin_readpassphrase found in the prefix"
        #endif

        #ifdef __has_builtin
            #if !__has_builtin(__builtin_readpassphrase)
                #error "__builtin_readpassphrase not found"
            #endif
        #elif ! defined(readpassphrase)
            __builtin_readpassphrase;
        #endif
        return 0;
        }
-----------

Code:

        int main(void) {

        /* With some toolchains (MSYS2/mingw for example) the compiler
         * provides various builtins which are not really implemented and
         * fall back to the stdlib where they aren't provided and fail at
         * build/link time. In case the user provides a header, including
         * the header didn't lead to the function being defined, and the
         * function we are checking isn't a builtin itself we assume the
         * builtin is not functional and we just error out. */
        #if !1 && !defined(_fseeki64) && !0
            #error "No definition for __builtin__fseeki64 found in the prefix"
        #endif

        #ifdef __has_builtin
            #if !__has_builtin(__builtin__fseeki64)
                #error "__builtin__fseeki64 not found"
            #endif
        #elif ! defined(_fseeki64)
            __builtin__fseeki64;
        #endif
        return 0;
        }
-----------
